A few design notes:</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Normal pieces</strong> — a deep-colored disc with concentric ring detailing and a subtle highlight. Red pieces use a dark crimson base (<code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem] inline-flex items-center h-5"><span class="inline-block w-3 h-3 border-[0.5px] border-border-200 rounded-[4px] flex-shrink-0 shadow-sm mr-1 align-middle" style="background-color: rgb(139, 0, 0);"></span>#8b0000</code>) with bright red rings; black pieces use near-black with gray rings.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>King pieces</strong> — two variants are shown:</p>
<ul class="[li_&amp;]:mb-0 [li_&amp;]:mt-1 [li_&amp;]:gap-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ul]:pb-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ol]:pb-1 list-disc flex flex-col gap-1 pl-8 mb-3">
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2"><strong>♛ symbol + gold dot ring</strong> — the queen glyph centered on the piece, surrounded by five evenly-spaced gold dots on the inner ring. Clean and readable.</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2"><strong>Polygon crown</strong> — a geometric 5-point crown shape with orb caps and a base bar, all in gold (<code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem] inline-flex items-center h-5"><span class="inline-block w-3 h-3 border-[0.5px] border-border-200 rounded-[4px] flex-shrink-0 shadow-sm mr-1 align-middle" style="background-color: rgb(255, 215, 0);"></span>#FFD700</code>). More "game-y" feel.</li>
</ul>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Both king styles use gold so they pop against both square colors regardless of piece color.</p>

opacity: 1; transform: none;"><div class="contents"><div data-is-streaming="false" class="group relative relative pb-3" style="opacity: 1; transform: none;"><h2 class="sr-only select-none">Claude respondió: Great question for a checkers project.</h2><div class="font-claude-response relative leading-[1.65rem] [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:bg-bg-000/50 [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:border-0.5 [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:border-border-400 [&amp;_.ignore-pre-bg&gt;div]:bg-transparent [&amp;_.standard-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pl-2 [&amp;_.standard-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,ul,ol,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pr-8 [&amp;_.progressive-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pl-2 [&amp;_.progressive-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,ul,ol,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pr-8"><div><div class="grid grid-rows-[auto_auto] min-w-0"><div class="row-start-1 col-start-1 min-w-0"><div class="min-w-0 pl-2 py-1.5"><div class="flex items-center gap-2"><button class="group/status flex items-center gap-2 py-1 text-sm transition-colors cursor-pointer text-left text-text-500 hover:text-text-300 flex-1 min-w-0" aria-expanded="false"><div class="inline-flex items-center gap-1 min-w-0" style="opacity: 1;"><span class="truncate text-sm font-base">Identified checkers AI question and prepared algorithmic recommendations</span><span class="inline-flex transition-transform duration-200 shrink-0 -rotate-90"><div style="width: 12px; height: 12px; display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center;"><svg width="16" height="16" viewBox="0 0 20 20" fill="currentColor" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" style="flex-shrink: 0;"><path d="M14.128 7.165a.502.502 0 0 1 .744.67l-4.5 5-.078.07a.5.5 0 0 1-.666-.07l-4.5-5-.06-.082a.501.501 0 0 1 .729-.656l.075.068L10 11.752z"></path></svg></div></span></div></button></div><span class="sr-only" role="status" aria-live="polite">Identified checkers AI question and prepared algorithmic recommendations</span><div class="grid transition-[grid-template-rows] duration-300 ease-out" style="grid-template-rows: 0fr;"><div class="overflow-hidden min-w-0"></div></div></div></div><div class="row-start-2 col-start-1 relative grid isolate min-w-0"><div class="row-start-1 col-start-1 relative z-[2] min-w-0"><div><div class="standard-markdown grid-cols-1 grid [&amp;_&gt;_*]:min-w-0 gap-3 standard-markdown"><p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Great question for a checkers project. Here are the main options, roughly in order of complexity:</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Minimax (bare)</strong>
The classic. Recursively explores all possible moves, assuming both players play optimally. Simple to implement, but explodes exponentially past depth ~4-5 without pruning. Good starting point to understand the problem.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Minimax + Alpha-Beta Pruning</strong>
The standard go-to for checkers. Prunes branches that can't possibly affect the result, letting you search roughly twice as deep for the same cost. At depth 8-10 with a decent evaluation function it plays very strong checkers. This is what most hobby and educational checkers AIs use.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Negamax</strong>
Just a cleaner way to write minimax — exploits the zero-sum symmetry so you write one function instead of two. Alpha-beta pruning drops right in. Highly recommended over raw minimax purely for code simplicity.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Negamax + Iterative Deepening</strong>
You run negamax at depth 1, then 2, then 3, etc., until your time budget runs out. The shallow searches finish fast and seed move ordering for the next depth, which makes alpha-beta prune far more aggressively. This is the practical upgrade that turns a decent AI into a strong one.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>MTD(f)</strong>
A more advanced search driver that calls a zero-window alpha-beta search repeatedly, converging on the true minimax value. Often faster than plain iterative deepening in practice. Worth considering once you have the basics solid.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>A strong evaluation function matters more than the algorithm.</strong> For checkers specifically you want to score: piece count, king count, board position (center/back rank control), piece mobility, and endgame patterns. A shallow search with a great eval beats a deep search with a naive one.</p>
<hr class="border-border-200 border-t-0.5 my-3 mx-1.5">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>My recommendation for a checkers game:</strong></p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Start with <strong>negamax + alpha-beta + iterative deepening</strong>. It's well-understood, deterministic, has tons of reference implementations, and produces genuinely challenging play. Add a transposition table (caching previously seen positions with a Zobrist hash) once that's working and you'll get another big strength boost essentially for free.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Want me to sketch out the core algorithm in code, or help design the evaluation function?</p></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div><div class="flex justify-start opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 group-focus-within:opacity-100 transition" role="group" aria-label="Message actions"><div class="text-text-300"><div class="text-text-300 flex items-stretch justify-between"><button type="button" data-cds="Button" class="cds-reset group/btn relative isolate inline-flex shrink-0 items-center justify-center gap-1.5 whitespace-nowrap select-none border-0 outline-none rounded h-control font-sans text-body font-medium [&amp;:disabled:not([aria-busy])]:opacity-50 disabled:pointer-events-none transition-shadow duration-fast focus-visible:shadow-focus text-primary aria-pressed:text-accent aspect-square w-control px-0  !text-muted hover:!text-primary" aria-label="Copiar" data-testid="action-bar-copy"><span aria-hidden="true" class="absolute -z-[1] rounded-[inherit] transition-colors duration-fast group-focus-visible/btn:shadow-[inset_0_0_0_1px_var(--cds-page-bg)] bg-transparent group-hover/btn:bg-fill-ghost-hover group-aria-expanded/btn:bg-fill-ghost-hover inset-0 group-aria-pressed/btn:bg-accent group-hover/btn:group-aria-pressed/btn:bg-accent cds-btn-squish "></span><span class="inline-flex items-center gap-1 "><span class="relative"><span data-cds="Icon" class="transition-all opacity-100 scale-100" aria-hidden="true" style="font-family: var(--font-anthropicons, Anthropicons-Variable); font-feature-settings: &quot;liga&quot; 0; font-optical-sizing: auto; font-style: normal; font-variation-settings: normal; line-height: 1; width: 1em; height: 1em; display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; flex-shrink: 0; user-select: none; font-size: 20px; font-weight: 433.3;"></span><span data-cds="Icon" class="absolute inset-0 transition-all opacity-0 scale-50" aria-hidden="true" style="font-family: var(--font-anthropicons, Anthropicons-Variable); font-feature-settings: &quot;liga&quot; 0; font-optical-sizing: auto; font-style: normal; font-variation-settings: normal; line-height: 1; width: 1em; height: 1em; display: flex; align-items: center; opacity: 1; transform: none;"><div class="contents"><div data-is-streaming="false" class="group relative relative pb-3" style="opacity: 1; transform: none;"><h2 class="sr-only select-none">Claude respondió: Several clean levers you can pull, and they compose well together:</h2><div class="font-claude-response relative leading-[1.65rem] [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:bg-bg-000/50 [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:border-0.5 [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:border-border-400 [&amp;_.ignore-pre-bg&gt;div]:bg-transparent [&amp;_.standard-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pl-2 [&amp;_.standard-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,ul,ol,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pr-8 [&amp;_.progressive-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pl-2 [&amp;_.progressive-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,ul,ol,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pr-8"><div><div class="standard-markdown grid-cols-1 grid [&amp;_&gt;_*]:min-w-0 gap-3 standard-markdown"><p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Several clean levers you can pull, and they compose well together:</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Search depth</strong> — the most direct knob. Cap the iterative deepening at a max depth (e.g. easy = 2, medium = 5, hard = 10). Shallower search means the AI misses long-term consequences. Simple and very effective.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Time budget</strong> — instead of a fixed depth, give the AI a time limit (e.g. 0.1s / 0.5s / 3s). With iterative deepening this is natural — just stop when the clock runs out. Feels more organic than a hard depth cap.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Evaluation function weakening</strong> — deliberately make the eval noisier at lower difficulties:</p>
<ul class="[li_&amp;]:mb-0 [li_&amp;]:mt-1 [li_&amp;]:gap-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ul]:pb-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ol]:pb-1 list-disc flex flex-col gap-1 pl-8 mb-3">
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">Add a random ±N bonus to each position score. Small N = minor mistakes, large N = erratic play.</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">Omit evaluation terms entirely (e.g. easy mode ignores positional scoring, only counts material).</li>
</ul>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Move selection randomness</strong> — instead of always picking the best move, use an epsilon-greedy approach: with probability ε pick a random legal move, otherwise pick the best one. Easy = ε of 0.3, hard = ε of 0. This makes low difficulty feel more human and less robotic.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Aspiration window narrowing</strong> — relevant if you use MTD(f) or aspiration search; wider windows at low depth naturally produce weaker play.</p>
<hr class="border-border-200 border-t-0.5 my-3 mx-1.5">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>What works best in practice:</strong></p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">For a polished feel, combine two levers — a depth cap <em>plus</em> a small random noise on the eval score at easy/medium. Pure depth reduction alone makes the AI play obviously shortsighted; the noise makes it feel like it just "misjudged" rather than being clearly blind. Something like:</p>
<div class="overflow-x-auto w-full px-2 mb-6"><table class="min-w-full border-collapse text-sm leading-[1.7] whitespace-normal"><thead class="text-left"><tr><th scope="col" class="text-text-100 border-b-0.5 border-border-300/60 py-2 pr-4 align-top font-bold">Difficulty</th><th scope="col" class="text-text-100 border-b-0.5 border-border-300/60 py-2 pr-4 align-top font-bold">Max depth</th><th scope="col" class="text-text-100 border-b-0.5 border-border-300/60 py-2 pr-4 align-top font-bold">Eval noise</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">Easy</td><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">2</td><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">±80 pts</td></tr><tr><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">Medium</td><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">5</td><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">±20 pts</td></tr><tr><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">Hard</td><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">9</td><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">0</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">The exact numbers depend on your scoring scale, but the shape holds.</p>

opacity: 1; transform: none;"><div class="contents"><div data-is-streaming="false" class="group relative relative pb-3" style="opacity: 1; transform: none;"><h2 class="sr-only select-none">Claude respondió: Minimax with a depth cap of 1 or 2.</h2><div class="font-claude-response relative leading-[1.65rem] [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:bg-bg-000/50 [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:border-0.5 [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:border-border-400 [&amp;_.ignore-pre-bg&gt;div]:bg-transparent [&amp;_.standard-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pl-2 [&amp;_.standard-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,ul,ol,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pr-8 [&amp;_.progressive-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pl-2 [&amp;_.progressive-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,ul,ol,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pr-8"><div><div class="standard-markdown grid-cols-1 grid [&amp;_&gt;_*]:min-w-0 gap-3 standard-markdown"><p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Minimax with a depth cap of 1 or 2.</strong></p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">At depth 1 it just looks at every legal move, scores the resulting board, and picks the best one — no recursion that goes anywhere meaningful. It's essentially "take the best-looking move right now without thinking ahead." A handful of comparisons per turn.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">If you add the random noise on top (which costs almost nothing), it also plays believably badly at low difficulty without needing any extra logic.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Alpha-beta pruning is worth adding even here — it's only about 10-15 extra lines and makes the code cleaner for when you want to scale up later — but at depth 1-2 it barely matters since the tree is tiny anyway.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">So the simplest viable stack is:</p>
<ul class="[li_&amp;]:mb-0 [li_&amp;]:mt-1 [li_&amp;]:gap-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ul]:pb-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ol]:pb-1 list-disc flex flex-col gap-1 pl-8 mb-3">
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">Minimax, depth 1-2</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">Basic eval (just count pieces + kings, weight kings ~1.5x a normal piece)</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">Optional ±random noise for difficulty flavor</li>
</ul>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">That's maybe 50-80 lines of code total and runs instantaneously. Want me to write it out?</p></div></div></div></div></div><div class="flex justify-start opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 group-focus-within:opacity-100 transition" role="group" aria-label="Message actions"><div class="text-text-300"><div class="text-text-300 flex items-stretch justify-between"><button type="button" data-cds="Button" class="cds-reset group/btn relative isolate inline-flex shrink-0 items-center justify-center gap-1.5 whitespace-nowrap select-none border-0 outline-none rounded h-control font-sans text-body font-medium [&amp;:disabled:not([aria-busy])]:opacity-50 disabled:pointer-events-none transition-shadow duration-fast focus-visible:shadow-focus text-primary aria-pressed:text-accent aspect-square w-control px-0  !text-muted hover:!text-primary" aria-label="Copiar" data-testid="action-bar-copy"><span aria-hidden="true" class="absolute -z-[1] rounded-[inherit] transition-colors duration-fast group-focus-visible/btn:shadow-[inset_0_0_0_1px_var(--cds-page-bg)] bg-transparent group-hover/btn:bg-fill-ghost-hover group-aria-expanded/btn:bg-fill-ghost-hover inset-0 group-aria-pressed/btn:bg-accent group-hover/btn:group-aria-pressed/btn:bg-accent cds-btn-squish "></span><span class="inline-flex items-center gap-1 "><span class="relative"><span data-cds="Icon" class="transition-all opacity-100 scale-100" aria-hidden="true" style="font-family: var(--font-anthropicons, Anthropicons-Variable); opacity: 1; transform: none;"><div class="contents"><div data-is-streaming="false" class="group relative relative pb-3" style="opacity: 1; transform: none;"><h2 class="sr-only select-none">Claude respondió: For truly unbeatable checkers, the answer is actually a solved one:</h2><div class="font-claude-response relative leading-[1.65rem] [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:bg-bg-000/50 [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:border-0.5 [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:border-border-400 [&amp;_.ignore-pre-bg&gt;div]:bg-transparent [&amp;_.standard-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pl-2 [&amp;_.standard-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,ul,ol,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pr-8 [&amp;_.progressive-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pl-2 [&amp;_.progressive-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,ul,ol,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pr-8"><div><div class="standard-markdown grid-cols-1 grid [&amp;_&gt;_*]:min-w-0 gap-3 standard-markdown"><p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">For <strong>truly unbeatable</strong> checkers, the answer is actually a solved one:</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Checkers (8×8 English draughts) was completely solved in 2007 by Jonathan Schaeffer's team at the University of Alberta.</strong> Their program <strong>Chinook</strong> proved that perfect play from both sides always results in a draw. So an "unbeatable" opponent would draw against a perfect human and win against anything less.</p>
<hr class="border-border-200 border-t-0.5 my-3 mx-1.5">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Your practical options:</strong></p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Negamax + alpha-beta + iterative deepening + transposition table</strong>
At depth 15-20 with a strong eval it's effectively unbeatable by any human. Not mathematically perfect but nobody will beat it. This is the realistic ceiling for a self-built AI.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Chinook's endgame database (tablebases)</strong>
Schaeffer's team built lookup tables for all positions with 10 or fewer pieces — perfect play guaranteed in any endgame. You can download these databases and query them when the board thins out, then use your search for the opening/midgame. This is how strong checkers engines work in practice.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>The full Chinook database</strong>
The complete solution is ~500GB. It covers every reachable position perfectly. Plug it in and the AI literally cannot lose. Overkill for most projects but it exists.</p>
<hr class="border-border-200 border-t-0.5 my-3 mx-1.5">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Realistic recommendation:</strong></p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Negamax + alpha-beta + iterative deepening + a transposition table (Zobrist hashing) gets you to "unbeatable in practice" without needing a 500GB database. Plug in the endgame tablebases for positions under 10 pieces if you want the mathematically perfect endgame on top.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Want me to sketch out the transposition table implementation?</p></div></div></div></div></div><div class="flex justify-start opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 group-focus-within:opacity-100 transition" role="group" aria-label="Message actions"><div class="text-text-300"><div class="text-text-300 flex items-stretch justify-between"><button type="button" data-cds="Button" class="cds-reset group/btn relative isolate inline-flex shrink-0 items-center justify-center gap-1.5 whitespace-nowrap select-none border-0 outline-none rounded h-control font-sans text-body font-medium [&amp;:disabled:not([aria-busy])]:opacity-50 disabled:pointer-events-none transition-shadow duration-fast focus-visible:shadow-focus text-primary aria-pressed:text-accent aspect-square w-control px-0  !text-muted hover:!text-primary" aria-label="Copiar" data-testid="action-bar-copy"><span aria-hidden="true" class="absolute -z-[1] rounded-[inherit] transition-colors duration-fast group-focus-visible/btn:shadow-[inset_0_0_0_1px_var(--cds-page-bg)] bg-transparent group-hover/btn:bg-fill-ghost-hover group-aria-expanded/btn:bg-fill-ghost-hover inset-0 group-aria-pressed/btn:bg-accent group-hover/btn:group-aria-pressed/btn:bg-accent cds-btn-squish "></span><span class="inline-flex items-center gap-1 "><span class="relative"><span data-cds="Icon" class="transition-all opacity-100 scale-100" aria-hidden="true" style="font-family: var(--font-anthropicons, Anthropicons-Variable); font-feature-settings: &quot;liga&quot; 0; font-optical-sizing: auto; font-style: normal; font-variation-settings: normal; line-height: 1; width: 1em; height: 1em; display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; flex-shrink: 0; user-select: none; font-size: 20px; font-weight: 433.3;"></span><span data-cds="Icon" class="absolute inset-0 transition-all opacity-0 scale-50" aria-hidden="true" style="font-family: var(--font-anthropicons, Anthropicons-Variable); font-feature-settings: &quot;liga&quot; 0; font-optical-sizing: auto; font-style: normal; font-variation-settings: normal; line-height: 1; width: 1em; height: 1em; display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; flex-shrink: 0; user-select: none; font-size: 20px; font-weight: 433.3;"></span></span></span></button><div class="flex items-center"></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div><div class="[content-visibility:auto] [contain-intrinsic-size:auto_400px] pb-8 -mb-8 print:[content-visibility:visible]"><div data-test-render-count="2"><div class="group" style="height: auto; opacity: 1; transform: none;"><div class="contents"><div data-is-streaming="false" class="group relative relative pb-3" style="opacity: 1; transform: none;"><h2 class="sr-only select-none">Claude respondió: A* is a pathfinding algorithm — it's designed to find the shortest path between two points in a graph (think navigation, maze solving).</h2><div class="font-claude-response relative leading-[1.65rem] [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:bg-bg-000/50 [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:border-0.5 [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:border-border-400 [&amp;_.ignore-pre-bg&gt;div]:bg-transparent [&amp;_.standard-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pl-2 [&amp;_.standard-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,ul,ol,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pr-8 [&amp;_.progressive-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pl-2 [&amp;_.progressive-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,ul,ol,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pr-8"><div><div class="standard-markdown grid-cols-1 grid [&amp;_&gt;_*]:min-w-0 gap-3 standard-markdown"><p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">A* is a pathfinding algorithm — it's designed to find the shortest path between two points in a graph (think navigation, maze solving). It's not really a fit for checkers because:</p>
<ul class="[li_&amp;]:mb-0 [li_&amp;]:mt-1 [li_&amp;]:gap-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ul]:pb-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ol]:pb-1 list-disc flex flex-col gap-1 pl-8 mb-3">
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">In checkers there's no "destination" to navigate toward — you're trying to maximize an outcome against an opponent, not find a path from A to B.</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">A* has no concept of an adversary. It assumes the environment is neutral or cooperative, not that something is actively working against you.</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">It doesn't handle the two-player zero-sum nature of the game at all.</li>
</ul>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">So technically you <em>could</em> shoehorn it in — model game states as nodes and "cost" as board evaluation — but you'd just end up reinventing minimax in a roundabout way, and worse, without the adversarial logic it would ignore what the opponent does entirely.</p>
<hr class="border-border-200 border-t-0.5 my-3 mx-1.5">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><em><em>Where A</em> actually shines:</em>*</p>
<ul class="[li_&amp;]:mb-0 [li_&amp;]:mt-1 [li_&amp;]:gap-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ul]:pb-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ol]:pb-1 list-disc flex flex-col gap-1 pl-8 mb-3">
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">Pathfinding on a map (RPGs, strategy games, navigation)</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">Puzzle solving where there's one player and a clear goal state (15-puzzle, Sokoban)</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">Any problem where you're minimizing cost to reach a target</li>
</ul>
<hr class="border-border-200 border-t-0.5 my-3 mx-1.5">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>The honest answer:</strong> whoever suggested A* for checkers may have been thinking loosely about tree search in general — A*, minimax, and Monte Carlo are all forms of tree/graph search, so people sometimes conflate them. But for a two-player adversarial game, minimax-family algorithms are the right tool. A* isn't viable here in any meaningful sense.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Stick with negamax — it's the right abstraction for the problem.</p></div></div></div></div></div><div class="flex justify-start opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 group-focus-within:opacity-100 transition" role="group" aria-label="Message actions"><div class="text-text-300"><div class="text-text-300 flex items-stretch justify-between"><button type="button" data-cds="Button" class="cds-reset group/btn relative isolate inline-flex shrink-0 items-center justify-center gap-1.5 whitespace-nowrap select-none border-0 outline-none rounded h-control font-sans text-body font-medium [&amp;:disabled:not([aria-busy])]:opacity-50 disabled:pointer-events-none transition-shadow duration-fast focus-visible:shadow-focus text-primary aria-pressed:text-accent aspect-square w-control px-0  !text-muted hover:!text-primary" aria-label="Copiar" data-testid="action-bar-copy"><span aria-hidden="true" class="absolute -z-[1] rounded-[inherit] transition-colors duration-fast group-focus-visible/btn:shadow-[inset_0_0_0_1px_var(--cds-page-bg)] bg-transparent group-hover/btn:bg-fill-ghost-hover group-aria-expanded/btn:bg-fill-ghost-hover inset-0 group-aria-pressed/btn:bg-accent group-hover/btn:group-aria-pressed/btn:bg-accent cds-btn-squish "></span><span class="inline-flex items-center gap-1 "><span class="relative"><span data-cds="Icon" class="transition-all opacity-100 scale-100" aria-hidden="true" style="font-family: var(--font-anthropicons, Anthropicons-Variable); justify-content: center; flex-shrink: 0; user-select: none; font-size: 20px; font-weight: 433.3;"></span><span data-cds="Icon" class="absolute inset-0 transition-all opacity-0 scale-50" aria-hidden="true" style="font-family: var(--font-anthropicons, Anthropicons-Variable); transform: none;"><div class="flex flex-row gap-2 relative"><div class="flex-1"><div data-testid="user-message" class="font-large !font-user-message py-0.5 grid grid-cols-1 gap-2 relative [&amp;_ul]:!space-y-0 [&amp;_ol]:!space-y-0 [&amp;_ul]:pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:pl-8" style="max-height: none; overflow: hidden; position: relative;"><p class="whitespace-pre-wrap break-words">ok, i am told it will be implemented as a microservice where a backend sends the data to the AI in a parameter and the response is basically "Move piece X to Y". I am thinking using a json as a parameter with: team of AI, size of board, string with pieces "#r#r#r#r-" as they are at that moment in the board. String because given the size it should resolve the characters into a board.</p></div></div></div></div><div class="flex justify-start opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 group-focus-within:opacity-100 transition" role="group" aria-label="Message actions"><div class="text-text-300"><div class="text-text-300 flex items-stretch justify-between"><span class="text-text-500 text-xs flex items-center mr-2">26 may</span><div class="flex items-center"><button type="button" data-cds="Button" class="cds-reset group/btn relative isolate inline-flex shrink-0 items-center justify-center gap-1.5 whitespace-nowrap select-none border-0 outline-none rounded h-control font-sans text-body font-medium [&amp;:disabled:not([aria-busy])]:opacity-50 disabled:pointer-events-none transition-shadow duration-fast focus-visible:shadow-focus text-primary aria-pressed:text-accent aspect-square w-control px-0  !text-muted hover:!text-primary" aria-label="Reintentar"><span aria-hidden="true" class="absolute -z-[1] rounded-[inherit] transition-colors duration-fast group-focus-visible/btn:shadow-[inset_0_0_0_1px_var(--cds-page-bg)] bg-transparent group-hover/btn:bg-fill-ghost-hover group-aria-expanded/btn:bg-fill-ghost-hover inset-0 group-aria-pressed/btn:bg-accent group-hover/btn:group-aria-pressed/btn:bg-accent cds-btn-squish "></span><span class="inline-flex items-center gap-1 "><span data-cds="Icon" aria-hidden="true" style="font-family: var(--font-anthropicons, Anthropicons-Variable); opacity: 1; transform: none;"><div class="contents"><div data-is-streaming="false" class="group relative relative pb-3" style="opacity: 1; transform: none;"><h2 class="sr-only select-none">Claude respondió: That's a clean design.</h2><div class="font-claude-response relative leading-[1.65rem] [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:bg-bg-000/50 [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:border-0.5 [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:border-border-400 [&amp;_.ignore-pre-bg&gt;div]:bg-transparent [&amp;_.standard-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pl-2 [&amp;_.standard-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,ul,ol,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pr-8 [&amp;_.progressive-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pl-2 [&amp;_.progressive-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,ul,ol,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pr-8"><div><div class="standard-markdown grid-cols-1 grid [&amp;_&gt;_*]:min-w-0 gap-3 standard-markdown"><p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">That's a clean design. A few thoughts and suggestions:</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>What you have works well.</strong> Passing the board as a flat string is simple, stateless, and easy to serialize. The microservice stays pure — no stored state, same input always produces same output (important for determinism and testing).</p>
<hr class="border-border-200 border-t-0.5 my-3 mx-1.5">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Suggested JSON input:</strong></p>
<div role="group" aria-label="Código en json" tabindex="0" class="relative group/copy bg-bg-000/50 border-0.5 border-border-400 rounded-lg focus:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-accent-100"><div class="sticky opacity-0 group-hover/copy:opacity-100 group-focus-within/copy:opacity-100 top-2 py-2 h-12 w-0 float-right"><div class="absolute right-0 h-8 px-2 items-center inline-flex z-10"><button class="inline-flex

<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Where the character key might be:</p>
<ul class="[li_&amp;]:mb-0 [li_&amp;]:mt-1 [li_&amp;]:gap-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ul]:pb-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ol]:pb-1 list-disc flex flex-col gap-1 pl-8 mb-3">
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2"><code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">#</code> — dark square (playable, empty)</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2"><code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">-</code> — light square (not playable)</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2"><code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">r</code> — red normal, <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">R</code> — red king</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2"><code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">b</code> — black normal, <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">B</code> — black king</li>
</ul>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Row separator could be <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">-</code> between rows, or just a fixed stride based on <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">board_size</code> (every N characters = one row). Fixed stride is simpler since you already know the board size.</p>
<hr class="border-border-200 border-t-0.5 my-3 mx-1.5">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Suggested JSON output:</strong></p>
<div role="group" aria-label="Código en json" tabindex="0" class="relative group/copy bg-bg-000/50 border-0.5 border-border-400 rounded-lg focus:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-accent-100"><div class="sticky opacity-0 group-hover/copy:opacity-100 group-focus-within/copy:opacity-100 top-2 py-2 h-12 w-0 float-right"><div class="absolute right-0 h-8 px-2 items-center inline-flex z-10"><button class="inline-flex

</span></span><span><span>  </span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(244, 123, 133);">"from"</span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(234, 236, 240);">:</span><span> </span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(211, 215, 222);">[</span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(94, 237, 237);">2</span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(211, 215, 222);">,</span><span> </span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(94, 237, 237);">3</span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(211, 215, 222);">]</span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(211, 215, 222);">,</span><span>
</span></span><span><span>  </span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(244, 123, 133);">"to"</span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(234, 236, 240);">:</span><span> </span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(211, 215, 222);">[</span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(94, 237, 237);">3</span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(211, 215, 222);">,</span><span> </span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(94, 237, 237);">4</span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(211, 215, 222);">]</span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(211, 215, 222);">,</span><span>
</span></span><span><span>  </span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(244, 123, 133);">"captures"</span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(234, 236, 240);">:</span><span> </span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(211, 215, 222);">[</span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(211, 215, 222);">[</span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(94, 237, 237);">2</span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(211, 215, 222);">,</span><span> </span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(94, 237, 237);">4</span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(211, 215, 222);">]</span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(211, 215, 222);">]</span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(211, 215, 222);">,</span><span>
</span></span><span><span>  </span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(244, 123, 133);">"resulting_board"</span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(234, 236, 240);">:</span><span> </span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(155, 233, 99);">"#r#r#r#r..."</span><span>
</span></span><span><span></span><span class="token token" style="color: rgb(211, 215, 222);">}</span></span></code></pre></div></div>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Returning the <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">resulting_board</code> after the move is a nice touch — the backend doesn't have to recompute it, and it acts as a sanity check.</p>
<hr class="border-border-200 border-t-0.5 my-3 mx-1.5">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>A few things worth nailing down:</strong></p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Coordinate system</strong> — decide early whether <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">[row, col]</code> is zero-indexed from the top-left or bottom-left, and document it. Mismatches here cause subtle bugs that are painful to track down.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Multi-jump moves</strong> — in checkers a piece can capture multiple times in one turn. Your <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">captures</code> array handles this, but make sure the input format can also <em>represent</em> mid-jump states if you ever need to resume a partial turn.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Invalid board handling</strong> — the microservice should return a clear error if the board string length doesn't match <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">board_size²</code>, or if it's the given team's turn but they have no legal moves (game over condition).</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Whose turn it is</strong> — you're passing <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">team</code> which covers this, good.</p>
<hr class="border-border-200 border-t-0.5 my-3 mx-1.5">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Overall the design is solid. The flat string board is a well-worn approach — Chinook and most checkers engines use a similar internal bitmask representation, and your string maps cleanly onto that. <p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Fully specified so an agent won't guess:</strong> the board string format with exact character keys, coordinate system, all JSON request/response shapes, HTTP status codes, Mongoose schema, environment variables, and Docker Compose structure are all explicit.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Shared package as the foundation</strong> — board parsing, move generation, and game-over detection live in one place and get imported by both the web service and the AI service, avoiding duplicated logic.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Phased build order</strong> — each phase has clear deliverables and tests before the next begins. This prevents an agent from building a UI before the engine is proven correct.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Difficulty system</strong> is wired in from the start — depth cap + eval noise table is baked into the AI spec so it doesn't need to be retrofitted later.</p>

<ul class="[li_&amp;]:mb-0 [li_&amp;]:mt-1 [li_&amp;]:gap-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ul]:pb-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ol]:pb-1 list-disc flex flex-col gap-1 pl-8 mb-3">
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2"><strong>1a.</strong> Define all types in <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">packages/shared/src/types.ts</code></li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2"><strong>1b.</strong> Implement board utilities in <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">packages/shared/src/board.ts</code></li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2"><strong>1c.</strong> Write unit tests for all board utility functions</li>
</ul>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">So the very first step is <strong>1a — defining the TypeScript types</strong>: <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">Team</code>, <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">PieceType</code>, <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">Cell</code>, <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">Move</code>, and <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">GameOverResult</code>. Everything else in the project depends on these being locked down first.</p></div></div></div></div></div><div class="flex justify-start opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 group-focus-within:opacity-100 transition" role="group" aria-label="Message actions"><div class="text-text-300"><div class="text-text-300 flex items-stretch justify-between"><button type="button" data-cds="Button" class="cds-reset group/btn relative isolate inline-flex shrink-0 items-center justify-center gap-1.5 whitespace-nowrap select-none border-0 outline-none rounded h-control font-sans text-body font-medium [&amp;:disabled:not([aria-busy])]:opacity-50 disabled:pointer-events-none transition-shadow duration-fast focus-visible:shadow-focus text-primary aria-pressed:text-accent aspect-square w-control px-0  !text-muted hover:!text-primary" aria-label="Copiar" data-testid="action-bar-copy"><span aria-hidden="true" class="absolute -z-[1] rounded-[inherit] transition-colors duration-fast group-focus-visible/btn:shadow-[inset_0_0_0_1px_var(--cds-page-bg)] bg-transparent group-hover/btn:bg-fill-ghost-hover group-aria-expanded/btn:bg-fill-ghost-hover inset-0 group-aria-pressed/btn:bg-accent group-hover/btn:group-aria-pressed/btn:bg-accent cds-btn-squish "></span><span class="inline-flex items-center gap-1 "><span class="relative"><span data-cds="Icon" class="transition-all opacity-100 scale-100" aria-hidden="true" style="font-family: var(--font-anthropicons, Anthropicons-Variable); font-feature-settings: &quot;liga&quot; 0; font-optical-sizing: auto; font-style: normal; font-variation-settings: normal; line-height: 1; width: 1em; height: 1em; display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; flex-shrink: 0; user-select: none; font-size: 20px; font-weight: 433.3;"></span><span data-cds="Icon" class="absolute inset-0 transition-all opacity-0 scale-50" aria-hidden="true" style="font-family: var(--font-anthropicons, Anthropicons-Variable); font-feature-settings: &quot;liga&quot; 0; font-optical-sizing: auto; font-style: normal; font-variation-settings: normal; line-height: 1; width: 1em; height: 1em; display: flex; align-items: center; opacity: 1; transform: none;"><div class="contents"><div data-is-streaming="false" class="group relative relative pb-3" style="opacity: 1; transform: none;"><h2 class="sr-only select-none">Claude respondió: Great instinct.</h2><div class="font-claude-response relative leading-[1.65rem] [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:bg-bg-000/50 [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:border-0.5 [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:border-border-400 [&amp;_.ignore-pre-bg&gt;div]:bg-transparent [&amp;_.standard-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pl-2 [&amp;_.standard-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,ul,ol,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pr-8 [&amp;_.progressive-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pl-2 [&amp;_.progressive-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,ul,ol,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pr-8"><div><div class="standard-markdown grid-cols-1 grid [&amp;_&gt;_*]:min-w-0 gap-3 standard-markdown"><p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Great instinct. Board logic is exactly the kind of thing where an LLM can write plausible-looking code that passes its own tests but has subtle bugs — mandatory capture enforcement, multi-jump chains, promotion mid-jump, edge columns. Those are easy to get slightly wrong in ways unit tests won't catch if the tests were written by the same agent that wrote the code.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>A few practical suggestions:</strong></p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Add a visual debugger step to the PRD</strong>
Before any game UI exists, have the agent build a minimal standalone HTML file that renders a board string visually. The agent drops a board string in, it renders the board, highlights legal moves for a clicked piece, and shows what the board looks like after a move. No React, no server — just a single file you open in a browser. You verify the logic is correct with your own eyes in 5 minutes.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Canonical test positions</strong>
Give the agent specific board strings with known correct answers that <em>you</em> define, not the agent. For example:</p>
<ul class="[li_&amp;]:mb-0 [li_&amp;]:mt-1 [li_&amp;]:gap-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ul]:pb-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ol]:pb-1 list-disc flex flex-col gap-1 pl-8 mb-3">
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">"This position has exactly one mandatory capture, verify it is the only move returned"</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">"This multi-jump captures 3 pieces, verify the resulting board string"</li>
</ul>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">If the agent writes both the positions and the expected answers it can rationalize its own bugs. You defining them closes that loop.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Split authorship</strong>
Instruct the agent to write the board utilities first, then in a <em>separate prompt</em> write the tests without looking at the implementation — only at the type signatures and the rules spec in the PRD. This mimics test-driven development and reduces the chance the tests just mirror the code's assumptions.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Log every move in plain English</strong>
Have the agent add a <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">describeMove()</code> utility that returns a human-readable string like <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">"red normal at [2,3] captures black at [3,4], lands at [4,5], promoted to king"</code>. Log this to the console during tests so you can scan the output and spot nonsense without reading board strings.</p>
<hr class="border-border-200 border-t-0.5 my-3 mx-1.5">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">The visual debugger is the highest-value suggestion — it costs the agent maybe 30 minutes to build and gives you a direct window into the logic before anything else is built on top of it. Want me to add a <strong>Phase 0 — Visual Board Debugger</strong> step to the PRD?</p></div></div></div></div></div><div class="flex justify-start opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 group-focus-within:opacity-100 transition" role="group" aria-label="Message actions"><div class="text-text-300"><div class="text-text-300 flex items-stretch justify-between"><button type="button" data-cds="Button" class="cds-reset group/btn relative isolate inline-flex shrink-0 items-center justify-center gap-1.5 whitespace-nowrap select-none border-0 outline-none rounded h-control font-sans text-body font-medium [&amp;:disabled:not([aria-busy])]:opacity-50 disabled:pointer-events-none transition-shadow duration-fast focus-visible:shadow-focus text-primary aria-pressed:text-accent aspect-square w-control px-0  !text-muted hover:!text-primary" aria-label="Copiar" data-testid="action-bar-copy"><span aria-hidden="true" class="absolute -z-[1] rounded-[inherit] transition-colors duration-fast group-focus-visible/btn:shadow-[inset_0_0_0_1px_var(--cds-page-bg)] bg-transparent group-hover/btn:bg-fill-ghost-hover group-aria-expanded/btn:bg-fill-ghost-hover inset-0 group-aria-pressed/btn:bg-accent group-hover/btn:group-aria-pressed/btn:bg-accent cds-btn-squish "></span><span class="inline-flex items-center gap-1 "><span class="relative"><span data-cds="Icon" class="transition-all opacity-100 scale-100" aria-hidden="true" style="font-family: var(--font-anthropicons, Anthropicons-Variable); <p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Re-implementation as a verification strategy</strong> — by asking the agent to rewrite the logic in plain JS from scratch rather than just importing it, any bugs in the TypeScript will likely surface as discrepancies between the two versions, or get caught when the test positions don't behave as described.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>You define the expected outcomes</strong> — all six test positions have explicit expected results written by a human (you, via this prompt), not the agent. The agent can't rationalize its way past "exactly 1 legal move."</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>The discrepancy banner</strong> — if the agent notices something ambiguous in the board utilities while reimplementing them, it's forced to surface it visually rather than silently pick an interpretation.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>The checklist at the end</strong> — gives you a concrete sign-off artifact. Don't proceed to Phase 2 until every box is ticked.</p></div></div><div class="flex flex-col gap-2 py-2"><div class="group/artifact-block relative flex text-left font-ui rounded-lg overflow-hidden overflow: hidden; position: relative;"><p class="whitespace-pre-wrap break-words">Visual Debugger Plan - Clarification Questions
Reading the detailed prompt, I have some questions before finalizing the plan:
1. 
Location: The prompt says project root, but you mentioned "tests folder". Which location do you prefer:
- 
/board-debugger.html (project root - as specified)
- 
/test/board-debugger.html (tests folder)
2. 
Re-implementing TypeScript in JavaScript: The prompt requires vanilla JS re-implementation of the TypeScript logic. This creates duplication. Would you prefer:
- 
Re-implement as specified (standalone, no build)
- 
Use Bun to compile TypeScript to a single JS bundle and include that
3. 
Test Position Coordinates: I need to verify the board string encodings. For "Single capture":
- 
Row 2 col 3 = index 2*8+3 = 19 → 'r'
- 
Row 3 col 4 = index 3*8+4 = 28 → 'b'
- 
Row 4 col 5 = index 4*8+5 = 37 → '#' (empty dark square)
- 
Do you want me to verify and document all coordinate math?
4. 
Character encoding for positions: The prompt shows test positions as readable descriptions - should I:
- 
Compute exact 64-character strings with all light squares as '-'
- 
Include the calculation as comments in the HTML file
Would you like me to proceed with the plan assuming:
- 
Root location (board-debugger.html)
- 
Vanilla JS re-implementation (no build)
- 
Full coordinate math documented in comment</p><div class="absolute bottom-0 left-0 right-0 h-12 bg-gradient-to-t from-bg-300 to-transparent pointer-events-none"></div></div><button class="pb-3 pt-1 text-xs text-text-500/80 hover:text-text-100 transition w-3/4 text-left rounded-lg">Mostrar más</button></div></div></div><div class="flex justify-start opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 group-focus-within:opacity-100 transition" role="group" aria-label="Message actions"><div class="text-text-300"><div class="text-text-300 flex items-stretch justify-between"><span class="text-text-500 text-xs flex items-center mr-2">26 may</span><div class="flex items-center"><button type="button" data-cds="Button" class="cds-reset group/btn relative isolate inline-flex shrink-0 items-center justify-center gap-1.5 whitespace-nowrap select-none border-0 outline-none rounded h-control font-sans text-body font-medium [&amp;:disabled:not([aria-busy])]:opacity-50 disabled:pointer-events-none transition-shadow duration-fast focus-visible:shadow-focus text-primary aria-pressed:text-accent aspect-square w-control px-0  !text-muted hover:!text-primary" aria-label="Reintentar"><span aria-hidden="true" class="absolute -z-[1] rounded-[inherit] transition-colors duration-fast group-focus-visible/btn:shadow-[inset_0_0_0_1px_var(--cds-page-bg)] bg-transparent group-hover/btn:bg-fill-ghost-hover group-aria-expanded/btn:bg-fill-ghost-hover inset-0 group-aria-pressed/btn:bg-accent group-hover/btn:group-aria-pressed/btn:bg-accent cds-btn-squish "></span><span class="inline-flex items-center gap-1 "><span data-cds="Icon" aria-hidden="true" style="font-family: var(--font-anthropicons, Anthropicons-Variable); opacity: 1; transform: none;"><div class="contents"><div data-is-streaming="false" class="group relative relative pb-3" style="opacity: 1; transform: none;"><h2 class="sr-only select-none">Claude respondió: Good questions from the agent.</h2><div class="font-claude-response relative leading-[1.65rem] [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:bg-bg-000/50 [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:border-0.5 [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:border-border-400 [&amp;_.ignore-pre-bg&gt;div]:bg-transparent [&amp;_.standard-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pl-2 [&amp;_.standard-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,ul,ol,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pr-8 [&amp;_.progressive-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pl-2 [&amp;_.progressive-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,ul,ol,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pr-8"><div><div class="grid grid-rows-[auto_auto] min-w-0"><div class="row-start-1 col-start-1 min-w-0"><div class="min-w-0 pl-2 py-1.5"><div class="flex items-center gap-2"><button class="group/status flex items-center gap-2 py-1 text-sm transition-colors cursor-pointer text-left text-text-500 hover:text-text-300 flex-1 min-w-0" aria-expanded="false"><div class="inline-flex items-center gap-1 min-w-0" style="opacity: 1;"><span class="truncate text-sm font-base">Synthesized clarification questions to guide decisive implementation choices</span><span class="inline-flex transition-transform duration-200 shrink-0 -rotate-90"><div style="width: 12px; height: 12px; display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center;"><svg width="16" height="16" viewBox="0 0 20 20" fill="currentColor" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" style="flex-shrink: 0;"><path d="M14.128 7.165a.502.502 0 0 1 .744.67l-4.5 5-.078.07a.5.5 0 0 1-.666-.07l-4.5-5-.06-.082a.501.501 0 0 1 .729-.656l.075.068L10 11.752z"></path></svg></div></span></div></button></div><span class="sr-only" role="status" aria-live="polite">Synthesized clarification questions to guide decisive implementation choices</span><div class="grid transition-[grid-template-rows] duration-300 ease-out" style="grid-template-rows: 0fr;"><div class="overflow-hidden min-w-0"></div></div></div></div><div class="row-start-2 col-start-1 relative grid isolate min-w-0"><div class="row-start-1 col-start-1 relative z-[2] min-w-0"><div><div class="standard-markdown grid-cols-1 grid [&amp;_&gt;_*]:min-w-0 gap-3 standard-markdown"><p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Good questions from the agent. Here are clean answers to give it:</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>1. Location → Project root</strong>
<code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">/board-debugger.html</code> as specified. It's a dev tool, not a test artifact, and it needs to be dead simple to open — no navigating into folders.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>2. Re-implementation → Vanilla JS, no build</strong>
The entire point is zero dependencies and instant open-in-browser. A Bun compile step defeats that. The duplication is intentional — it's the verification mechanism, not a bug.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>3. Coordinate math → Yes, verify and document all of it</strong>
The agent got the Single Capture math right. It should do the same for every test position and leave the calculations as comments in the file. That's how a human reviewer can spot a wrong board string without counting characters manually.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>4. Character encoding → Compute exact 64-character strings AND show the math</strong>
Both. The exact string is what runs, the comment is what a human checks. Neither alone is sufficient.</p>
<hr class="border-border-200 border-t-0.5 my-3 mx-1.5">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">So your reply to the agent should be:</p>
<blockquote class="ml-2 border-l-4 border-border-300/10 pl-4 text-text-300">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Proceed with: root location, vanilla JS re-implementation, full coordinate math in comments, exact 64-character strings for all test positions. Your coordinate math for Single Capture is correct — apply the same verification to all remaining test positions before writing the strings.</p>

But there are some real bugs and a few spec gaps worth flagging before you sign off on it.</p>
<hr class="border-border-200 border-t-0.5 my-3 mx-1.5">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>🔴 Critical Bugs</strong></p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>1. King Moves — piece on a light square</strong>
The board string places the king at index 36 → <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">[4,4]</code>. But <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">isPlayableSquare(4,4)</code> = <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">(4+4)%2 = 0</code> → that's a <strong>light square</strong>. The king is on a non-playable cell. The string needs to shift to index 35 (<code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">[4,3]</code>) or 37 (<code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">[4,5]</code>), both of which are dark squares.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>2. <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">isGameOver</code> — missing the "no moves = loss" case</strong>
Lines 229–232 only return <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">draw</code> when <em>both</em> teams have no moves. It never returns a winner when just one team is stuck. Standard checkers rules say if your team has no legal moves, you lose. Fix:</p>

<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>3. Active team never switches after a move</strong>
<code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">makeMove()</code> applies the move and re-renders the board, but <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">activeTeam</code> never flips. The checklist item <em>"after red moves, active team auto-switches to black"</em> will fail. Add to <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">makeMove()</code>:</p>

<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Also update the radio button UI to match.</p>
<hr class="border-border-200 border-t-0.5 my-3 mx-1.5">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>🟡 Spec Gaps</strong></p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>4. No coordinate math comments</strong>
The prompt explicitly required each board string to have its index math documented as a comment (e.g. <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">// r at index 19 → row 2, col 3</code>). None of the test position functions have these. This is the human-verifiable paper trail for catching wrong strings.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>5. Warning banner never fires</strong>
The <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">#warningBanner</code> element exists in the HTML but no code ever populates or shows it. The spec asked the agent to flag any discrepancy it found between the TypeScript source and the PRD during reimplementation.</p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>6. No Moves position also on a light square</strong>
<code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">b</code> at index 0 → <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">[0,0]</code> → <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">(0+0)%2 = 0</code> → light square. It still produces the right test result (red has no pieces → black wins), but the piece renders on a non-playable cell which is visually misleading. Move the <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">b</code> to index 1 (<code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">[0,1]</code>) which is dark.</p>
<hr class="border-border-200 border-t-0.5 my-3 mx-1.5">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>✅ What's Working Well</strong></p>
<ul class="[li_&amp;]:mb-0 [li_&amp;]:mt-1 [li_&amp;]:gap-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ul]:pb-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ol]:pb-1 list-disc flex flex-col gap-1 pl-8 mb-3">
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2"><code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">parseBoardString</code> / <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">serializeBoardString</code> round-trip logic is correct</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2"><code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">getLegalMoves</code> mandatory capture enforcement is correct (lines 199–200)</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">Multi-jump recursion logic looks sound</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2"><code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">applyMove</code> is clean and non-mutating</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2"><code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">describeMove</code> output is clear and readable</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">Move log with collapsible board-before/after is a nice touch</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">All test position coordinate math (Single Capture, Multi-Jump, Promotion) is correct</li>

<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>Send back to the agent with these specific fixes:</strong></p>
<blockquote class="ml-2 border-l-4 border-border-300/10 pl-4 text-text-300">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Fix the following before sign-off:</p>
<ol class="[li_&amp;]:mb-0 [li_&amp;]:mt-1 [li_&amp;]:gap-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ul]:pb-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ol]:pb-1 list-decimal flex flex-col gap-1 pl-8 mb-3">
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">King Moves board string: move king to index 35 ([4,3]) and add coordinate math comment</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">No Moves board string: move black piece to index 1 ([0,1]) and add coordinate math comment</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2"><code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">isGameOver</code>: add individual team no-moves loss conditions before the draw check</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2"><code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">makeMove</code>: flip <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">activeTeam</code> after applying the move and sync the radio button UI</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">Add coordinate math comments to all six test position board strings</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">Add at minimum a console.warn (or banner text) noting that no discrepancies were found during reimplementation</li>
</ol>

opacity: 1; transform: none;"><div class="contents"><div data-is-streaming="false" class="group relative relative pb-3" style="opacity: 1; transform: none;"><h2 class="sr-only select-none">Claude respondió: That's a sharp observation and you're right — the current Phase 1 design is implicitly hardcoded to English draughts.</h2><div class="font-claude-response relative leading-[1.65rem] [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:bg-bg-000/50 [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:border-0.5 [&amp;_pre&gt;div]:border-border-400 [&amp;_.ignore-pre-bg&gt;div]:bg-transparent [&amp;_.standard-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pl-2 [&amp;_.standard-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,ul,ol,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pr-8 [&amp;_.progressive-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pl-2 [&amp;_.progressive-markdown_:is(p,blockquote,ul,ol,h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)]:pr-8"><div><div class="standard-markdown grid-cols-1 grid [&amp;_&gt;_*]:min-w-0 gap-3 standard-markdown"><p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">That's a sharp observation and you're right — the current Phase 1 design is implicitly hardcoded to English draughts. If you want modularity, the architecture needs a rethink before anything else is built on top of it.</p>
<hr class="border-border-200 border-t-0.5 my-3 mx-1.5">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>What the current design assumes implicitly</strong></p>
<ul class="[li_&amp;]:mb-0 [li_&amp;]:mt-1 [li_&amp;]:gap-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ul]:pb-1 [&amp;:not(:last-child)_ol]:pb-1 list-disc flex flex-col gap-1 pl-8 mb-3">
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">Red moves down, black moves up — hardcoded direction</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">Normal pieces move one square diagonally forward only</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">Kings move one square in any diagonal direction</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">Mandatory capture always enforced</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">Promotion happens only at the back rank</li>
<li class="font-claude-response-body whitespace-normal break-words pl-2">Board is always square, 8x8 or 10x10</li>
</ul>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Every one of those is a <em>variant axis</em> — different checkers rulesets change them.</p>
<hr class="border-border-200 border-t-0.5 my-3 mx-1.5">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>The main ruleset variants you'd want to cover</strong></p>
<div class="overflow-x-auto w-full px-2 mb-6"><table class="min-w-full border-collapse text-sm leading-[1.7] whitespace-normal"><thead class="text-left"><tr><th scope="col" class="text-text-100 border-b-0.5 border-border-300/60 py-2 pr-4 align-top font-bold">Variant</th><th scope="col" class="text-text-100 border-b-0.5 border-border-300/60 py-2 pr-4 align-top font-bold">Key differences</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">English draughts (8x8)</td><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">Mandatory capture, short king, forward-only normals</td></tr><tr><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">International draughts (10x10)</td><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">Flying king (moves multiple squares), mandatory maximum capture</td></tr><tr><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">Brazilian (8x8)</td><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">International rules on a smaller board</td></tr><tr><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">Russian draughts</td><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">King is flying, captures can go backward mid-sequence</td></tr><tr><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">Turkish draughts</td><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">Pieces move orthogonally, not diagonally</td></tr><tr><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">Pool checkers</td><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">Captures can go backward for normal pieces</td></tr><tr><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">Custom</td><td class="border-b-0.5 border-border-300/30 py-2 pr-4 align-top">Player-defined rules</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<hr class="border-border-200 border-t-0.5 my-3 mx-1.5">
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]"><strong>The fix: a RuleSet configuration object</strong></p>
<p class="font-claude-response-body break-words whitespace-normal leading-[1.7]">Instead of hardcoding behavior in the move generator, every rule becomes a parameter. The board utilities accept a <code class="bg-text-200/5 border border-0.5 border-border-300 text-danger-000 whitespace-pre-wrap rounded-[0.4rem] px-1 py-px text-[0.9rem]">RuleSet</code> alongside the board, and all behavior is derived from it.</p>
<div role="group" aria-label="Código en typescript" tabindex="0" class="relative group/copy bg-bg-000/50 border-0.5 border-border-400 rounded-lg focus:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-accent-100"><div class="sticky opacity-0 group-hover/copy:opacity-100 group-focus-within/copy:opacity-100 top-2 py-2 h-12 w-0 float-right"><div class="absolute right-0 h-8 px-2 items-center inline-flex z-10"><button class="inline-flex

  disabled:shadow-none
  disabled:drop-shadow-none border-transparent
